MARKETS · THE WORLDWIDE GUIDE

THE WORLD'S INSURANCE COMPANIES

By Michael de la Force · Editor-in-Chief, LIKE® Magazine · Markets · October 2026

Insurance is the balance sheet behind almost everything else in these pages: the ships, the aircraft, the buildings, the films and the fortunes. This guide — the second in LIKE® Magazine's Markets department — maps the companies that carry that risk, house by house, at full depth.

The list covers 85 companies and groups in six categories: 15 life and health insurers, 14 property and casualty insurers, 14 composite and multiline groups, 35 reinsurance groups, 6 specialty carriers, and Lloyd's of London as a flagged marketplace reference row — a marketplace, not a carrier. The reinsurance roster follows the AM Best Top 50 Global Reinsurance Groups (the August 2026 edition for ranks 39 to 50 and the 2025 edition for ranks 26 to 38); property and casualty follows AM Best's U.S. ranking of March 20, 2026; composite and life rankings follow the Forbes Global 2000 of 2026.

For each house the guide gives the same full field set: founded; headquarters and offices; ownership, parent and listing; scale figures, dated and labeled by source; the full published leadership — chief executive, chair-level officers, chief financial officer, chief operating officer where the role exists, and senior leadership including the chief actuary where the house publishes one; clients or client categories where published; specialties and lines of business in full; and published contact details. Where a house does not publish a field, the entry says so plainly — not published, or not disclosed — rather than leaving the reader to guess. Named clients are not claimed anywhere: no house in this guide publishes a client roster for this use, so the clients field records client categories only.

Two 2026 changes anchor this edition. On October 1 and 2, 2026, Zurich Insurance Group completed its £8.1 billion all-cash acquisition of Beazley, which was delisted from the London Stock Exchange on October 2; Kristof Terryn serves as chief executive of Beazley and Zurich Global Specialty, subject to regulatory approval, and Helen Pickford as chief financial officer. At Berkshire Hathaway, Greg Abel has served as president and chief executive officer since January 1, 2026; Howard G. Buffett became chairman and Warren E. Buffett chairman emeritus on September 18, 2026, and Ajit Jain continues to lead the insurance operations. The guide was refreshed on October 22, 2026, and the newest entry records Assicurazioni Generali's board-approved restructure of October 21, 2026, which abolishes the general manager role and consolidates the group's markets into five regions from January 2027.

The named gaps are stated, not hidden. Twenty houses publish no named group chief financial officer; 18 publish no further named senior leadership beyond the chief executive and finance lead; 40 publish no headquarters switchboard number; and only four publish a general email address — Deutsche Rück, Peak Re, Africa Re and Taiping Re. TransRe's premiums are not separately disclosed by its parent. Premium and revenue figures are heterogeneous — gross written premiums, net written premiums, insurance revenue and total revenue, from fiscal 2024 to fiscal 2026 depending on the ranking edition and reporting cycle — and each figure is labeled in its entry; they are not directly comparable row to row.

LIFE AND HEALTH

1. AIA GROUP LIMITED — HONG KONG, CHINA

Founded 1919 · Life & health · Listed (HKEX)

Headquarters: Hong Kong, China

Region: Asia-Pacific

Website: www.aia.com

Headquarters phone: +852 2832 6166

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 46.9

Chief executive: Lee Yuan Siong, Executive Director, Group Chief Executive and President

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Garth Jones, Group Chief Financial Officer

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Sir Mark Edward Tucker, Independent Non-executive Chairman; Mitchell New, Group General Counsel; Pek-San Ang, Group Chief Human Resources Officer; Stuart Spencer, Group Chief Marketing Officer; Biswa Misra, Group Chief Technology Officer and Life Operations Officer

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Mark Konyn, Group Chief Investment Officer; Ben Ng, Group Chief Risk Officer (from 1 January 2026, succeeding Jayne Plunkett who retired); Jacky Chan (Wing-Shing Chan), Regional Chief Executive and Group Chief Distribution Officer; Tan Hak Leh, Regional Chief Executive; Leo Grepin, Regional Chief Executive and Group Chief Strategy Officer; Fisher Zhang, Regional Chief Executive

Key people: Lee Yuan Siong (Group Chief Executive and President); George Yeo (Independent Non-Executive Chairman)

Clients / client categories: Individuals and families across 18 Asia-Pacific markets; employers buying group insurance and employee benefits; bancassurance customers of partner banks

Specialties / lines of business: Individual life insurance; accident and health insurance; savings and investment-linked plans; group insurance and employee benefits; credit life; pension services

Offices: Hong Kong (headquarters); operations across 18 markets in Asia-Pacific including Mainland China, Singapore, Malaysia, Thailand, the Philippines, South Korea, Australia, New Zealand and a joint venture in India

Distinctiveness: AIA is the largest independent publicly listed pan-Asian life insurance group, with a presence in 18 markets and origins in Shanghai in 1919. Its ‘Premier Agency’ is its principal distribution channel — contributing 70% of AIA Hong Kong’s value of new business in 2025 — and AIA Hong Kong is ranked number one globally for MDRT members.

Access / how business is placed: Sold through tied Premier Agency forces, bancassurance partnerships (including long-term bank partnerships across markets), and IFA/broker channels, per company results materials. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: AIA was founded in 1919 by Cornelius Vander Starr in Shanghai as American Asiatic Underwriters; it became American International Assurance in 1948. It operated under AIG until the 2008 financial crisis, after which AIG divested its holding and AIA listed on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 2010. It is now the largest independent publicly listed pan-Asian life insurance group, operating in 18 markets across Asia-Pacific.

Notable notes: FY2025 total weighted premium income (TWPI) US$46.900bn.

Sources: https://www.hkexnews.hk/listedco/listconews/sehk/2026/0204/2026020400644.pdf (HKEX list of directors and their role and function, 4 February 2026); https://www.aia.com/en/media-centre/press-releases/2025/aia-group-press-release-20250918 (appointment of Ben Ng as Group Chief Risk Officer, 18 September 2025); https://www.reuters.com/legal/litigation/aia-groups-first-half-new-business-value-rises-10-2026-08-19/ (H1 2026 results and 18-market footprint, 19 August 2026)

Editorial notes: TWPI is a management volume measure, not IFRS insurance revenue. No currency conversion needed. | No president/COO role exists (Group Chief Executive and President is the single top role). Independent Non-executive Chairman changed from Edmund Tse to Sir Mark Edward Tucker (per HKEX list of directors, 4 February 2026). Jayne Plunkett retired as Group Chief Risk Officer 1 January 2026; Ben Ng succeeded her. Fresh (third-party reported, not from official source): AIA agreed ~September 2026 to acquire a 24.99% stake in China Post Life Insurance for RMB 12 billion. Sami Taipalus is reported by third parties as Group Chief Investor Relations Officer but this was not verified from an official source. | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

2. CNP ASSURANCES — ISSY-LES-MOULINEAUX, FRANCE

Founded 1959 · Life & health · Subsidiary of La Banque Postale

Headquarters: Issy-les-Moulineaux, France

Region: Europe

Website: www.cnp.fr

Headquarters phone: +33 1 42 18 88 88

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 44.2

Chief executive: Marie-Aude Thépaut, Chief Executive Officer (since Jan 11, 2024)

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Not published — no named group CFO is published by this house

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Véronique Weill, Chairwoman of the Board of Directors (term renewed April 2025, through 2029); Thomas Béhar, Group Chief Risk Officer and Deputy Chief Executive Officer; Sarah Bouquerel, Group Financial and Non-Financial Director and Deputy Chief Executive Officer; Sun Lee, Group General Counsel

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Olivier Guigné, Group Chief Investment Officer and Director of Artificial Intelligence, Innovation and Transformation; Thomas Chardonnel, Director of Property and Personal Protection Europe; François Guilgot, Director of Individual Savings and Pension Europe; Christophe Harrigan, Director of Social Protection Europe; Fouad Latrech, Group Director of Digital and Data Services

Key people: Véronique Weill (Chairwoman); Marie-Aude Thépaut (Chief Executive Officer)

Clients / client categories: Individuals and families buying life, health, annuity and savings cover; employers and groups buying group life, health and retirement benefits

Specialties / lines of business: Savings & pensions (traditional and unit-linked); personal risk/protection (term creditor insurance, group death/disability, health); property & casualty (via La Banque Postale integration); Brazil and Italy operations; bancassurance partnerships

Offices: Paris (headquarters)

Distinctiveness: CNP Assurances is France’s leading personal insurer and the single insurance vector of La Banque Postale, which has owned 100% of its capital since the June 2022 delisting; it completed a ‘full’ bancassurance merger with La Banque Postale’s insurance subsidiaries in April 2023. It distributes in France through La Banque Postale and BPCE (Banques Populaires/Caisses d’Epargne) under long-term agreements running to 2036 and 2030 respectively, and internationally through bank joint ventures such as Caixa in Brazil and Santander Consumer Finance.

Access / how business is placed: Sold via partner bank networks (La Banque Postale, BPCE) and its own Amétis network; group and creditor insurance is tailored for companies, local authorities, mutual insurers, non-profits and banks. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: CNP Assurances' predecessor funds date to 1850 and 1868; the Caisse Nationale de Prévoyance was created in 1959 by combining three French provident funds. It developed a bancassurance model through La Poste and the Caisses d'Epargne, and was integrated with La Banque Postale in 2020. It is France's leading personal insurer, operating in Europe and Latin America.

Notable notes: FY2025 premium income EUR39.1bn. 2025 events: launch of CNP Assurances Protection Sociale; disposals of CNP UniCredit Vita and CNP Cyprus Insurance Holdings; Brazil partnership with Embracon.

Sources: https://www.cnp.fr/en/the-cnp-assurances-group/who-we-are/the-governance/executive-committee; https://www.cnp.fr/en/the-cnp-assurances-group/newsroom/press-releases/2025/cnp-assurances-renews-veronique-weill-s-term-of-office-as-a-director-and-chairwoman-of-its-board-of-directors; https://live.euronext.com/en/node/12340511 (Thépaut appointment notice, Jan 11, 2024); https://www.cnp.fr/en/cnp/content/download/13983/file/CNPAssurances_holding_Management_Report_2025.pdf; https://www.franceassureurs.fr/abous-us/our-organization/

Editorial notes: Converted at Federal Reserve G.5A 2025 average EUR/USD 1.1306. servicepresse@cnp.fr is press-only, so contact_email left blank. Founded reflects 1959 creation of the Caisse Nationale de Prévoyance; predecessor funds date to 1850/1868. | Wholly-owned subsidiary of La Banque Postale (delisted June 2022); group-level executives sit at parent La Banque Postale (e.g., Stéphane Dedeyan, former CNP CEO, now chairs La Banque Postale's management board). No president/COO - CNP uses two Deputy CEOs (Béhar, Bouquerel). No standalone CFO title since Sept 2025: finance function led by Sarah Bouquerel (Group Financial and Non-Financial Director and Deputy CEO); Thomas Béhar moved from CFO to Group CRO in Sept 2025. CEO changed Jan 2024 (Dedeyan to Thépaut). Sold Italian subsidiary to UniCredit for €619M (June 2025); signed sale of CNP Cyprus Insurance Holdings to Hellenic Bank (July 2024). 'Lead for Impact' strategic plan. HQ-only (no global hub-city list published). | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

3. CHINA LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY LIMITED — BEIJING, CHINA

Founded 1949 · Life & health · State-controlled; listed (SSE/HKEX)

Headquarters: Beijing, China

Region: Asia-Pacific

Website: www.e-chinalife.com

Headquarters phone: +86 10 6363 3333

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 101.6

Chief executive: Li Mingguang, Executive Director and President

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Yuan Ying, Person in Charge of Finance

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Cai Xiliang, Chairman of the Board and Executive Director; Liu Hui, Executive Director, Vice President, Chief Investment Officer and Board Secretary; Ruan Qi, Executive Director, Vice President, Chief Risk Officer; Heng Victor Ja Wei, Company Secretary

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Hou Jin, Assistant to the President, Chief Actuary (since November 2023); Xu Chongmiao, Chief Compliance Officer and Person in Charge of Compliance; Zhang Xinyu, Assistant to the President, Chief Network Security Officer; Hu Zhijun, Person in Charge of Audit

Key people: Cai Xiliang (Chairman); Li Mingguang (President)

Clients / client categories: Individual customers across China (the group reported 1,495,000 exclusive individual agents serving them in its filings); group and institutional customers for pension and group business; bancassurance customers of partner banks

Specialties / lines of business: Individual life insurance (participating and non-participating); group life insurance; annuities; accident insurance; health insurance (critical illness, medical); pension business (China Life Pension); asset management (China Life Asset Management)

Offices: Beijing (headquarters, 16 Financial Street, Xicheng District); Hong Kong (China Life Insurance (Overseas) Company Limited)

Distinctiveness: China Life is China’s largest life insurer and a state-controlled group tracing its origins to 1949 (the listed entity was incorporated in 2003). Its SEC-filed materials describe an extensive distribution network of 1,495,000 exclusive individual agents, 85,000 direct sales representatives and 234,000 bancassurance sales representatives, plus telephone, internet and email channels.

Access / how business is placed: Sold through exclusive individual agents, direct sales representatives, bancassurance outlets, and telephone/internet/electronic channels, per company filings. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: China Life traces its origins to the founding of the People's Insurance Company of China in 1949. The modern listed group was created when China Life Insurance (Group) Company set up China Life Insurance Company Limited on June 30, 2003; it listed overseas in December 2003 and on the Shanghai Stock Exchange in January 2007. It is China's largest life insurer by premiums, a state-controlled group also active in asset management and overseas business.

Notable notes: FY2025 gross written premiums RMB729.887bn; total revenue RMB616.065bn.

Sources: https://CompaniesMarketCap.com/annual-reports/52.ar.en.2025.pdf (2025 Annual Report, approved by the Board 25 March 2026); https://pub.lbkrs.com/news-notices/hk/827080448194.PDF (HKEX announcement, convening of 2025 annual results briefing, 17 March 2026); https://pub.lbkrs.com/news-notices/hk/822502197076.PDF (HKEX announcement, resignation of Non-executive Director Wang Junhui, 22 January 2026)

Editorial notes: Converted at Federal Reserve G.5A 2025 average CNY/USD 7.1875. Founded reflects 1949 origins; listed entity incorporated 2003. ir@echinalife.com is investor-relations only, so contact_email left blank. AXA/Allianz/Aviva/Generali/Zurich/Ping An belong to the Composite slice, not duplicated here. | No president/COO role exists (President is the top executive role; Cai Xiliang is Chairman, not CEO). No CFO title published; finance function titled 'Person in Charge of Finance' (Yuan Ying, since July 2024). CFO therefore recorded with exact published title. Bai Kai (Vice President) resigned July 2025; Wang Junhui (Nonexecutive Director) resigned 22 January 2026. Employee Representative Director Li Wei appointed 11 March 2026. Li Mingguang is also Secretary of the Party Committee and was the company's former Chief Actuary. | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

4. DAI-ICHI LIFE HOLDINGS, INC. — TOKYO, JAPAN

Founded 1902 · Life & health · Listed (TSE)

Headquarters: Tokyo, Japan

Region: Asia-Pacific

Website: www.dai-ichi-life-hd.com/en

Headquarters phone: +81 3 3216 1222

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 46.4

Chief executive: Tetsuya Kikuta, Representative Director, President, Group Chief Executive Officer

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Taisuke Nishimura, Managing Executive Officer, Group Chief Financial Officer

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Seiji Inagaki, Director, Chair of the Board; Hitoshi Yamaguchi, Representative Director, Senior Managing Executive Officer, Group Chief Human Resources Officer; Takako Kitahori, Director, Managing Executive Officer, Group Chief Customer Experience Officer (Japan); Stephen Barnham, Senior Managing Executive Officer, Group Chief Information Officer & Group Chief Digital Officer; Webster Coates, Managing Executive Officer, Group Chief Compliance Officer; Hirotaka Noji, Expert Executive Officer, Group Chief Accounting Officer; Figen Ulgen, Executive Officer, Group Chief Data and AI Officer; Kaori Sakamoto, Executive Officer, Group Chief Brand and Culture Officer; Yukiko Sakai, Executive Officer, Group Chief Sustainability Officer; Hiroko Ishii, Executive Officer, Group Chief Internal Audit Officer

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Hideyuki Ohashi, Managing Executive Officer, Group Chief Risk Officer; Takashi Iida, Managing Executive Officer, Business Head, Asset Management; Kazuyuki Shigemoto, Managing Executive Officer (responsible for Fixed Income Investment and Alternative Investment); Katsushi Makiuchi, Executive Officer (responsible for Real Estate Asset Management, Corporate and Structured Finance); Akifumi Kai, Managing Executive Officer, Business Head, International Life Insurance; Satoshi Takemoto, Managing Executive Officer, Chief General Manager, North America; Brett Clark, Senior Managing Executive Officer, Chief General Manager, Asia Pacific; Hironori Takahashi, Managing Executive Officer, Business Head, Insurance (Japan); Kentaro Ogata, Managing Executive Officer, Business Head, New Fields of Business; Hiroyuki Kanou, Senior Managing Executive Officer (matters regarding Daiichi Frontier Life; concurrently Representative Director, President of Daiichi Frontier Life Insurance Co., Ltd.)

Key people: Seiji Inagaki (Director, Chairman of the Board); Tetsuya Kikuta (Representative Director, President and Group CEO)

Clients / client categories: Individuals and families buying life, health, annuity and savings cover; employers and groups buying group life, health and retirement benefits

Specialties / lines of business: Domestic life insurance (Dai-ichi Life: life, medical, nursing-care); savings-type insurance (Dai-ichi Frontier Life); annuities; group pensions; overseas life insurance (Protective Life US, TAL Australia, Asia); asset management

Offices: Tokyo (headquarters)

Distinctiveness: Dai-ichi Life Holdings is Japan’s third-largest life insurer; it demutualized and listed on the Tokyo Stock Exchange on April 1, 2010. It pursues a multi-brand, multi-channel strategy: Dai-ichi Life (tied agents), Dai-ichi Frontier Life (bancassurance via bank tellers), and Neo Life (direct internet sales), with overseas operations including TAL in Australia and Protective Life in the US.

Access / how business is placed: Tied agents consult customers at workplaces and homes; savings products are sold through bank tellers (Dai-ichi Frontier Life); simpler products are sold directly online (Neo Life), per company strategy disclosures. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: Dai-ichi Life was founded on September 15, 1902, as Japan's first mutual life insurance company. It demutualized and listed on the Tokyo Stock Exchange in 2010 and adopted a holding-company structure in 2016. It has expanded internationally through Protective Life in the United States, TAL in Australia, and businesses across Asia.

Notable notes: FY ended March 31, 2026 consolidated premium and other income JPY6.944066tn; ordinary revenue JPY11.308275tn.

Sources: https://www.daiichilife-group.com/en/investor/news/assets/pdf/2026_index_007.pdf (Changes of Officers effective June 22, 2026; full Directors and Executive Officers roster)

Editorial notes: Converted at Federal Reserve G.5A 2025 average JPY/USD 149.5686. Seiji Inagaki confirmed as chair of the board effective June 22, 2026 in the official officer-change release. | No president/COO role exists (President and Group CEO is the top role; senior deputy is Representative Director, Senior Managing Executive Officer and Group CHRO Hitoshi Yamaguchi). No named chief actuary in the June 2026 officer release; actuarial matters sit under Group CFO Taisuke Nishimura. Fresh: company renamed from Dai-ichi Life Holdings, Inc. to Daiichi Life Group, Inc. effective 1 April 2026 (company-announced). The company publishes no hub-city office list; Tokyo only listed as HQ here. | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

5. LEGAL & GENERAL GROUP PLC — LONDON, UNITED KINGDOM

Founded 1836 · Life & health · Listed (LSE)

Headquarters: London, United Kingdom

Region: Europe

Website: group.legalandgeneral.com

Headquarters phone: Not published

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 9.5

Chief executive: António Simões, Group Chief Executive Officer

President / chief operating officer: Katie Worgan, Group Chief Operating Officer (from March 2025)

Chief financial officer: Andrew Kail, Group Chief Financial Officer (from December 2025)

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Scott Wheway, Chair; Chris Knight, Group Chief Risk Officer; Emma Holden, Chief People Officer (from February 2026); Maria Alvarez-Scott, Group General Counsel and Company Secretary Designate (from February 2026)

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Eric Adler, Chief Executive Officer, Asset Management; Laura Mason, Chief Executive Officer, Retail; Gareth Mee, Chief Executive Officer, Institutional Retirement (from December 2025)

Key people: Scott Wheway (Chairman); António Simões (Group Chief Executive)

Clients / client categories: Individuals and families buying life, health, annuity and savings cover; employers and groups buying group life, health and retirement benefits

Specialties / lines of business: Institutional retirement (pension risk transfer, bulk annuities); retail retirement (individual annuities, lifetime mortgages, workplace pensions); UK protection (life, critical illness, income protection); asset management (LGIM)

Offices: London (headquarters, One Coleman Street)

Distinctiveness: Legal & General is the UK pension risk transfer (PRT) market leader, writing £10.2bn of buy-in volume in 2025 for a 27% market share (IPE/LCP), including the £4.4bn British Airways pension buy-in — the largest ever UK bulk annuity. Its Institutional Retirement division also wrote record volumes in the US and Canada, and the group reported record UK retail annuity sales of £2bn in 2024.

Access / how business is placed: Institutional retirement business is placed directly with pension trustees via employee-benefit consultants; retail protection, annuity and retirement products are sold direct (digital, contact centres) and through financial advisers and intermediaries. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: Legal & General was founded in June 1836 in London by six lawyers as the New Law Life Assurance Society, initially serving the legal profession. It grew into a FTSE 100 financial services group spanning pensions, annuities, and asset management. In 2025-2026 it announced the sale of its US protection business to Meiji Yasuda and launched a 1.2bn GBP share buyback.

Notable notes: Announced sale of US protection business to Meiji Yasuda (2025); launched a 1.2bn GBP share buyback in 2026; Scott Wheway succeeded Sir John Kingman as chair after the May 21, 2026 AGM; announced ~1,000 job reductions by mid-2027 (September 2026).

Sources: https://group.legalandgeneral.com/about-us/our-management/; https://group.legalandgeneral.com/asset/494db4/globalassets/group/about-us/corporate-governance/goverance-report-2025.pdf (2025 Annual Report & Accounts, Group Management Committee); https://www.lse.co.uk/rns/legal-general-appoints-new-asset-management-ceo-dl5a4gdpt8ushdi.html (Eric Adler appointment); https://group.legalandgeneral.com/asset/493318/globalassets/group/newsroom/press-releases/pdfs/2025/09/lg-rns-september-2025.pdf (Kail CFO appointment release); https://careers.legalandgeneral.com/leadership-jobs

Editorial notes: Converted at Federal Reserve G.5A 2025 average GBP/USD 1.3192. Figure is insurance revenue GBP7.210bn (FY2025); total revenue GBP10.462bn. General corporate phone not verified. | Chair changed: Sir John Kingman succeeded by Scott Wheway (2026). CFO changed: Jeff Davies to Andrew Kail (Dec 2025); Davies joined Resolution Life as CFO (March 2026). L&G announced ~1,000 UK job cuts by mid-2026 (Sept 2026). Asset Management CEO Eric Adler joined from PGIM (Sept 2025, joined Dec; succeeds Michelle Scrimgeour). No chief actuary named in the Group Management Committee roster. Company does not publish a global hub-city list (HQ-only). | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

6. MANULIFE FINANCIAL CORPORATION — TORONTO, CANADA

Founded 1887 · Life & health · Listed (TSX/NYSE)

Headquarters: Toronto, Canada

Region: North America

Website: www.manulife.com

Headquarters phone: +1 416 926 3000

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 20.7

Chief executive: Phil Witherington, President and Chief Executive Officer (effective May 9, 2025)

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Colin Simpson, Chief Financial Officer

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Donald R. Lindsay, Chair of the Board; Shamus Weiland, Chief Technology & Operations Officer (from July 1, 2026); Jodie Wallis, Chief AI Officer (Executive Leadership Team, expanded scope including enterprise data); Sarah Chapman, Global Chief Marketing & Customer Experience Officer (effective Jan 1, 2027, joining Executive Leadership Team)

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Stephanie Fadous, Chief Actuary (also took on Inforce Management and Reinsurance, July 2026); Steve Finch, President & CEO, Manulife Asia (from May 9, 2025); Paul Lorentz, President & CEO, Global Wealth and Asset Management; Patrick Graham, President & CEO, Manulife Canada (from July 1, 2026); Wilton Kee, CEO, Manulife Hong Kong & Macau (from July 1, 2026)

Key people: Phil Witherington (President and CEO); Don Lindsay (Chair of the Board)

Clients / client categories: Individuals and families buying life, health, annuity and savings cover; employers and groups buying group life, health and retirement benefits

Specialties / lines of business: Asia (life, health, wealth); Canada (individual insurance, group benefits, wealth); US/John Hancock (life, long-term care, annuities, wealth); global wealth & asset management

Offices: Toronto (global headquarters)

Distinctiveness: Manulife describes itself in its 2025 MD&A as a top-three pan-Asian life insurer with a 125-plus-year history, operating across 12 Asian markets, Canada, and the US (as John Hancock). In Asia it fields over 100,000 contracted agents and more than 100 bank partnerships, including a regional exclusive bancassurance partnership with DBS Bank giving access to over 35 million bank customers.

Access / how business is placed: Products are distributed through brokers, independent agents, broker-dealers, banks, wholesalers, affinity partners, other third parties and its own Asian sales force, per its annual MD&A. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: The Manufacturers Life Insurance Company was incorporated on June 23, 1887, in Toronto and mutualized in 1968. It demutualized in 1999, creating Manulife Financial Corporation as the holding company, and merged with John Hancock in 2004. It operates as Manulife in Canada and Asia and as John Hancock in the United States.

Notable notes: FY2025 insurance revenue C$28.888bn; investment income C$19.014bn; other revenue C$8.129bn.

Company does not publish a global hub-city list (HQ-only). | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

7. MASSACHUSETTS MUTUAL LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY — SPRINGFIELD, UNITED STATES

Founded 1851 · Life & health · Mutual

Headquarters: Springfield, United States

Region: North America

Website: www.massmutual.com

Access / how business is placed: Distributed through tied agency networks, bancassurance partnerships with leading banks, and digital channels across its Asian and African markets, per company reports. No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed.

History: Prudential plc was founded on May 30, 1848, in Hatton Garden, London, as The Prudential Mutual Assurance Investment and Loan Association; its industrial insurance department opened in 1854. It built large UK and US (Jackson) businesses before refocusing on Asia and Africa. After demerging M&G in 2019 and Jackson in 2021, it is a pure-play Asia and Africa life and health insurer.

Notable notes: FY2025 insurance revenue US$11.080bn.

Distinctiveness: Berkshire Hathaway’s insurance operations — GEICO, Berkshire Hathaway Specialty Insurance, General Re and the Berkshire Hathaway Reinsurance Group — are the foundation of its business model: premiums collected before claims are paid create the insurance ‘float’ that funds Berkshire’s investments. GEICO is one of the largest US auto insurers; the group’s underwriting has consistently generated profits alongside float growth.

Access / how business is placed: GEICO sells direct to consumers (web, phone); Berkshire Hathaway Specialty Insurance distributes through retail and wholesale brokers; Gen Re and BH Reinsurance Group deal directly with cedants and via reinsurance brokers. Buffett began acquiring insurers with National Indemnity in 1967, acquired GEICO in full in 1996, and built the Berkshire Hathaway Reinsurance Group into a global reinsurer. Insurance is now Berkshire's core business: in the 2025 annual letter, CEO Greg Abel described insurance as the core that generates the float funding Berkshire's diversified investments.

Notable notes: Greg Abel became CEO Jan 1, 2026; Howard G. Buffett became chairman and Warren Buffett chairman emeritus Sept 2026 (official Sept 18, 2026 release). Nancy L. President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Clients / client categories: Members of the U.S. military community and their families — membership is restricted by military affiliation, as published by USAA

Distinctiveness: USAA (United Services Automobile Association) is a member-owned insurer founded in 1922 by 25 Army officers in San Antonio to serve the US military community, and it remains restricted to that community.

Access / how business is placed: Direct only — web, phone and mobile app — with membership restricted to active-duty service members, honorably discharged veterans, National Guard/Reserve members, officer candidates, and eligible family members (spouses, widows/widowers, children of members). No minimum premium is published — not publicly disclosed (membership, not premium, is the entry gate).

History: USAA was founded in 1922 in San Antonio by 25 U.S. Army officers who insured one another's automobiles when commercial insurers would not. It expanded into homeowners, life, banking and financial services for military members, veterans and their families. USAA is a member-owned reciprocal interinsurance exchange serving the military community.

Notable notes: Juan C. Andrade became President and CEO April 2025; David F. Melcher became chairman Aug 2026 succeeding James M. Zortman.

No minimum premium published — not publicly disclosed.

History: Roots in an 1824 Viennese mutual fire insurer; became Vienna Insurance Group as it expanded across Central and Eastern Europe after 1990. Listed in Vienna and Prague and majority held by Wiener Stadtische Versicherungsverein. The leading insurance group in CEE with around 50 insurers in more than 25 countries.

Notable notes: FY2025 GWP EUR16.3bn (+8.3%, all segments and lines growing, Life +8.9%); Feb 2026: Aon and KNIAZHA VIG announced a USD25m war-risk reinsurance facility with the US DFC (effective Feb 1, 2026) supporting Ukraine rebuilding.

Sources: https://group.vig/media/5xjdoeth/260415-ir-news-changes-to-the-managing-boards-of-vienna-insurance-group-wiener-staedtische-and-donau-versicherung.pdf;https://group.vig/en/investor-relations/ir-news/ir-meldungen/vienna-insurance-group-peter-thirring-takes-over-as-new-supervisory-board-chairman/;https://seekingalpha.com/article/4881877-vienna-insurance-group-ag-vnrfy-q4-2025-press-conference-call-transcript

Distinctiveness: India's national reinsurer and the only domestic reinsurer incorporated in India, majority state-owned and listed on the BSE and NSE since 2017. Under the Insurance Act, 1938, IRDAI designates GIC Re as the sole recipient of India's obligatory reinsurance cession — held at 4% of the sum insured on every general insurance policy for FY2026-27, giving it a guaranteed domestic book alongside its voluntary international business. It holds roughly two-thirds of India's gross reinsurance premium.

Access / how business is placed: Obligatory business is placed by statutory requirement: every Indian general insurer must cede 4% of each policy's sum insured to GIC Re. Voluntary treaty and facultative business is placed directly with its Mumbai head office or regional/branch offices, or through reinsurance brokers; cedants also use the company's published underwriting contacts. No minimum premium or treaty size is published; not publicly disclosed.

History: Incorporated Nov 22, 1972 under the Companies Act, 1956 as part of India's nationalization of general insurance; originally the holding company of four general insurers. Renotified as the Indian Reinsurer in November 2000; ceased to be a holding company in March 2003 under the GIBNA Amendment Act 2002; listed on BSE and NSE in 2017. India's national reinsurer with obligatory cessions from domestic insurers.

Notable notes: AM Best World's 50 Largest Reinsurers Aug 2026: #10 among non-IFRS 17 reporters. Hitesh Joshi confirmed Chairman-cum-Managing Director eff. June 16, 2026 (interim since Oct 1, 2025); Rajesh Laheri appointed CFO eff. June 18, 2026, succeeding V. Balkrishna. Government of India launched an offer for sale of up to 5% at INR352/share in June 2026. Record FY2025 profit of INR6,701.36 crore; domestic business ~75% of book. Building in-house AI assistant 'Ragini'. Board approved USD 49m solvency-restoration infusion into the Labuan branch (May 2026).

83. LANCASHIRE HOLDINGS LIMITED — HAMILTON, BERMUDA

Headquarters: Hamilton, Bermuda

Region: North America

Website: www.lancashiregroup.com

Headquarters phone: Not published

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 2.26 — FY2025 gross premiums written US$2,259.3m (+5.1% y/y); insurance revenue US$1,860.4m. H1 2026 GPW US$1,315.0m.

Chief executive: Alex Maloney (Group Chief Executive Officer)

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Natalie Kershaw (Group Chief Financial Officer)

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Not published

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Paul Gregory (Group Chief Underwriting Officer)

Key people: Alex Maloney (Group Chief Executive Officer); Philip Broadley (Non-Executive Chairman); Natalie Kershaw (Group Chief Financial Officer)

Clients / client categories: Businesses and specialist buyers placing specialty risks through brokers — see Specialties for the niches served

Specialties / lines of business: Specialty insurance: aviation; marine; energy; property; casualty (accident & health, professional lines); specialty insurance lines; reinsurance: casualty reinsurance (casualty, professional & financial lines, accident & health); property reinsurance (catastrophe-exposed, excess of loss); specialty reinsurance (aviation, marine, energy, property retrocession); Lloyd's market platform via Cathedral

Offices: Hamilton, Bermuda (headquarters — Power House, 7 Par-la-Ville Road); London, United Kingdom

Distinctiveness: A London-listed Bermuda/Lloyd's specialty insurer and reinsurer distinguished by explicit cycle management: it deliberately shrinks premium and returns capital in soft markets and scales up rapidly into hard markets, concentrating on short-tail, low-frequency/high-severity risks — property catastrophe, energy, aviation/aerospace, political risk and specialty reinsurance. It writes through a multi-platform architecture of Bermuda company paper, a UK carrier and Lloyd's syndicates, allowing brokers and clients to access the same underwriting through whichever platform fits regulatory and rating needs.

Access / how business is placed: Business is placed through brokers and intermediaries — Lloyd's syndicate business via the Lloyd's broker network, Bermuda/UK company paper via specialist brokers — and through direct specialty placements with corporates and cedents; it has no direct-to-consumer retail channel. No minimum premium or treaty size is published; not publicly disclosed.

History: Founded 2005 in Bermuda by Richard Brindle (formerly of Lloyd's) as a specialty (re)insurer; listed on the London Stock Exchange. Acquired Lloyd's insurer Cathedral Capital Limited for £266m in November 2013, establishing a Lloyd's platform (Cathedral syndicates 2010 and 3010). Brindle retired in April 2014 and was succeeded as Group CEO by Alex Maloney.

Notable notes: LSE: LRE, FTSE 250 constituent. FY2025: profit after tax US$293.4m, ROE 20.9%, discounted combined ratio 83.7%. S&P Global Ratings upgraded its long-term issuer credit and financial strength ratings from A- to A in 2025. Over US$3.7bn returned to shareholders since inception, including >100% of profits over 2024-25. Chair Philip Broadley (previously chair for seven years before moving to Hiscox in 2025) purchased shares in Sept 2026.

Sources: https://www.lancashiregroup.com/content/dam/lancashire/corporate2020/Investors/Results_Report_Presentations/2025/LHL-results-for-the-year-ended-31-December-2025.pdf; https://www.sharecast.com/equity/Lancashire_Holdings_Limited/profile; https://www.lse.co.uk/rns/LRE/acquisition-of-cathedral-capital-limited-completed-kvewe07v5jzinr8.html; https://www.insurancetimes.co.uk/lancashire-group-founder-and-chief-exec-richard-brindle-to-retire/1407949.article; https://cdn.prod.nntech.io/company-events/reports/18e431e4-755b-35fa-b49f-3ef04b695322/report.pdf

Editorial notes: GAPS (left blank): general corporate phone and email not verified from official disclosures (Sharecast lists +1 441 278 8950 but it is a third-party profile); president/COO, CRO, CIO, chief actuary not published by the company; Australia office (listed by third-party profiles) not verified from official disclosures. FRESH FACTS: interim dividend 7.5 cents for H1 2026; Q3 2026 trading statement due Nov 4, 2026. | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).

84. PELAGOS INSURANCE CAPITAL LIMITED (FORMERLY FIDELIS INSURANCE HOLDINGS LIMITED) — PEMBROKE, BERMUDA

Founded 2015 · Specialty · Listed (NYSE: PLGO); founded 2015 with private-equity backing from Crestview Partners, CVC Capital Partners and Pine Brook.

Headquarters: Pembroke, Bermuda

Region: North America

Website: www.pelagosinsurancecapital.com

Headquarters phone: Not published

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): 4.72 — FY2025 gross premiums written US$4,717.6m (insurance segment US$3,756.3m; reinsurance segment US$961.3m; +7.1% y/y). H1 2026 GPW +6.6% y/y.

Chief executive: Dan Burrows (Group Chief Executive Officer)

President / chief operating officer: Not published — no separate president/COO role is published for this house

Chief financial officer: Allan Decleir (Group Chief Financial Officer)

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Not published

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Jonny Strickle (Group Managing Director); Justin Michael Pearson (Chief Risk Officer); Allen Jason Kittinger (Chief Operating Officer); Ka'ulu'okala Nicole Kapu-Leyland (Group General Counsel)

Key people: Dan Burrows (Group Chief Executive Officer); Allan Decleir (Group Chief Financial Officer)

Clients / client categories: Businesses and specialist buyers placing specialty risks through brokers — see Specialties for the niches served

Specialties / lines of business: Specialty insurance: property (direct & facultative); marine; asset-backed finance and portfolio credit; energy; cyber; aviation & aerospace; political risk, violence & terrorism; reinsurance: property catastrophe reinsurance, retrocession and whole-account; capital-allocation model deploying capital through a network of underwriting partners

Offices: Pembroke, Bermuda (headquarters)

Distinctiveness: Bermuda-based Fidelis Insurance Holdings Limited rebranded as Pelagos Insurance Capital Limited effective May 11, 2026 (NYSE ticker changed from FIHL to PLGO), describing itself as a strategic capital allocator and risk selector in specialty insurance and reinsurance rather than a conventional balance-sheet carrier. Its model links third-party capital — including a 10-year rolling binder with The Fidelis Partnership, an MGA-style underwriting partner writing $5.4bn of premium across its capacity providers in 2025 — to specialist underwriting opportunities.

Access / how business is placed: Brokers, clients and underwriting partners deal with Pelagos through its underwriting offices in Bermuda, London and other hubs; the company states its arrangements with underwriters, brokers, clients and investors are unchanged by the rebrand. No minimum premium or treaty/account size is published; not publicly disclosed.

History: Founded 2015 in Bermuda by Richard Brindle and Neil McConachie (both formerly of Lancashire Holdings) as Fidelis Insurance Holdings Limited, launching with ~$1.5bn of capital from Crestview Partners, CVC Capital Partners, Pine Brook and other investors on a 'total return' capital-allocation model. Listed on the NYSE in June 2023 (FIHL). Rebranded as Pelagos Insurance Capital Limited on May 11, 2026 following AGM approval; NYSE ticker changed from FIHL to PLGO on May 12, 2026.

Notable notes: NYSE: PLGO (renamed from NYSE: FIHL, May 2026). FY2025 net income US$225.5m (vs US$113.3m in 2024), combined ratio 94.8% (vs 99.7%); H1 2026 net income US$152.4m and book value per diluted share +9.1% YTD. AM Best affirmed financial strength A (Excellent), stable outlook, April 2026. Company describes itself as an 'expert capital allocator and risk selector in specialty insurance and reinsurance'.; Sep 14, 2026: Fidelis (The Fidelis Partnership) executive Michael Davern appointed to the Pelagos board (Class I, through the 2027 AGM) and to the Risk and Investment Committees, replacing Fidelis designee Charles Mathias (resigned eff. Aug 2, 2026); Davern's director compensation paid directly to The Fidelis Partnership; TFP owns ~9.9% of Pelagos (TipRanks/sec filing; insurancebusinessmag, Sep 2026).

Sources: https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260709871356/en/Pelagos-Insurance-Capital-Schedules-Second-Quarter-2026-Financial-Results-Conference-Call; https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260513293678/en/Pelagos-Insurance-Capital-Reports-Strong-First-Quarter-2026; https://www.insurancebusinessmag.com/us/news/breaking-news/fidelis-completes-rebrand-to-pelagos-insurance-capital-574727.aspx; https://www.reinsurancene.ws/improved-underwriting-results-in-2025-set-stage-for-fidelis-pelagos-rebrand/; https://www.reinsurancene.ws/pelagos-gwp-rises-more-than-6-for-q226-to-1-3bn/; https://insurancenewsnet.com/oarticle/fidelis-insurance-raises-approximately-1-5-billion-in-initial-capital-from-leading-investors-innovative-hybrid-model-designed-to-maximize-return-on-equity-2; https://www.insidermonkey.com/insider-trading/company/fidelis+insurance+holdings+ltd/1636639/?fullscreen=0

MARKETPLACE (REFERENCE)

85. LLOYD'S OF LONDON — LONDON, UNITED KINGDOM

Founded 1688 · Marketplace (reference) · marketplace (statutory society of members; not a stock company)

Headquarters: London, United Kingdom

Region: Europe

Website: www.lloyds.com

Headquarters phone: +44 20 7327 1000

Published email: Not published — the house publishes no general email; contact is via the official website or published role-specific addresses

Scale — premiums or revenue (US$bn, as labeled per row): premiums 18.5 (2024 gross premiums written, AM Best World's 50 Largest Reinsurers 2025 edition; #1 non-IFRS-17 filer in AM Best Aug 2026 edition on 2025 results)

Chief executive: Patrick Tiernan, Chief Executive Officer

President / chief operating officer: Stephane Flaquet, Chief Operating Officer

Chief financial officer: Jim Bichard, Chief Financial Officer

Other C-suite and chair-level leadership: Sir Charles Roxburgh, Chairman of Lloyd's; Rachel Turk, Chief of Market Performance; Caroline Sandeman-Allen, Chief of Market Oversight; Jonathan May, CEO of Lloyd's Insurance Company; Dawn Miller, Chief Commercial Officer and CEO, Americas; Nathan Adams, Chief People Officer; Claire Schrader, General Counsel; David Sansom, Chief Risk Officer; Guillermo Donadini, Chief Investment Officer (Treasury & Investment Management)

Senior leadership, including chief actuary and named specialists where published: Not published — no further named senior leadership is published by this house

Key people: Patrick Tiernan (Chief Executive); Sir Charles Roxburgh (Chair)

Clients / client categories: Not a carrier and has no policyholder clients of its own: buyers of cover are businesses and individuals whose risks are placed by Lloyd's brokers with syndicates managed by managing agents; capital is provided by market members

Specialties / lines of business: Specialty insurance and reinsurance marketplace (not a carrier): classes written through syndicates — property; casualty incl. liability, financial lines and D&O; marine, aviation and energy; specialty (cyber, crime, political risk, terrorism, fine art and specie, bloodstock, kidnap and ransom); reinsurance (incl. retrocession); structured solutions

Offices: London (One Lime Street, HQ); Singapore (largest hub outside London); Shanghai (Lloyd's Reinsurance Company (China) Ltd); Dubai (DIFC); plus regional offices covering Australia, China, Hong Kong, India, Japan, Mexico, and the Americas

Distinctiveness: The world's oldest continuously operating insurance marketplace: founded 1688 at Edward Lloyd's London coffee house, now a statutory society under Lloyd's Acts. Risk is underwritten by competing syndicates run by managing agents, backed by capital from members (corporates and individuals); the Corporation of Lloyd's licenses and supervises the market but does not itself underwrite. 335+ years of continuous operation; the largest global hub for specialty and reinsurance risk.

Access / how business is placed: Access is intermediated: risks are placed exclusively through Lloyd's-registered brokers, or via coverholders/delegated-authority MGAs approved by managing agents. The Corporation does not deal directly with policyholders. No published minimum premium or account size — not publicly disclosed.

History: Originates in Edward Lloyd's London coffee house (1688) and was incorporated by the Lloyd's Act of 1871. It is a statutory insurance and reinsurance marketplace where member syndicates underwrite specialty risk, not a conventional insurance company. AM Best's August 2026 ranking placed it #1 among non-IFRS 17 reporting reinsurers after its reinsurance premiums grew 7.1% before forex.

Notable notes: Patrick Tiernan became Chief Executive on 1 Jun 2025; Sir Charles Roxburgh became Chair on 1 May 2025.

Sources: https://www.lloyds.com/about-lloyds/governance/governance-structure/bios/patrick-tiernan; https://www.lloyds.com/about-lloyds/media-centre/press-releases/lloyds-appoints-stephane-flaquet-as-chief-operating-officer; https://www.theinsurer.com/ti/news/lloyds-names-pwcs-bichard-as-cfo-cliff-to-depart-corporation-2026-01-16/; https://reinsurancene.ws/lloyds-unveils-new-executive-team-as-tiernan-begins-role-as-ceo/

Editorial notes: No published general contact email. No direct-dial numbers published. | STRUCTURE: Lloyd's is a statutory society (insurance marketplace), not a stock company; the officers listed are of the Corporation of Lloyd's. CEO transition: Patrick Tiernan (ex-Chief of Markets) succeeded John Neal 1 June 2025. Chairman: Sir Charles Roxburgh (from 1 May 2025, succeeding Bruce Carnegie-Brown). CFO: Jim Bichard (ex-PwC global insurance leader) from April 2026, succeeding Alexandra Cliff (departed). CIO Guillermo Donadini succeeded Eleanor Bucks (per AM Best/press reporting, company spokesperson confirmed). senior_leadership blank: underwriting is performed by managing agents/syndicates, not the Corporation; the Corporation publishes no CUO/chief actuary/chief medical roles. Lloyd's publishes no single 'major offices' list; offices above from lloyds.com regional pages and Lloyd's press releases. AM Best Aug 2026: Lloyd's ranked #1 globally among non-IFRS-17 reporters. | Re-verified at the October 2026 refresh (Oct. 22, 2026).
